# Erdős problems: complete statement corpus

This file is a verbatim rendering of the 1,217-record SQLite snapshot
`/private/tmp/erdos_problems.db` from Erdős Navigator. The snapshot
contains 1,179 statement texts; records 1180–1217 have metadata but no
statement text in that frozen source. Statements and supplementary text
are preserved exactly as stored, including TeX and HTML `<br>` markers.

The statement source is the Erdős Problems site snapshot (as of
2026-02-01 in the database provenance table). Status, prize, tags, OEIS,
and formalization metadata come from the linked upstream databases.
This file is a corpus, not a claim that every record is currently open.

| `erdosproblems_site` | https://www.erdosproblems.com | `2026-02-01` | `2026-07-24T04:08:32+00:00` | `frozen` |

### Problem 1

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1
- Status: open
- Prize: $500
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/1.lean
- OEIS: ["A276661"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,N\}$ with $\lvert A\rvert=n$ is such that the subset sums $\sum_{a\in S}a$ are distinct for all $S\subseteq A$ then\[N \gg 2^{n}.\]
```

### Problem 4

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/4
- Status: proved
- Prize: $10000
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/4.lean

- Last edited: 23 January 2026

```text
Is it true that, for any $C>0$, there are infinitely many $n$ such that\[p_{n+1}-p_n> C\frac{\log\log n\log\log\log\log n}{(\log\log \log n)^2}\log n?\]
```

### Problem 12

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/12
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/12.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A$ be an infinite set such that there are no distinct $a,b,c\in A$ such that $a\mid (b+c)$ and $b,c>a$. Is there such an $A$ with\[\liminf \frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{N^{1/2}}>0?\]Does there exist some absolute constant $c>0$ such that there are always infinitely many $N$ with\[\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert<N^{1-c}?\]Is it true that\[\sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{n}<\infty?\]
```

### Problem 13

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/13
- Status: proved
- Prize: $100
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/13.lean
- OEIS: ["A002264"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,N\}$ be such that there are no $a,b,c\in A$ such that $a\mid(b+c)$ and $a<\min(b,c)$. Is it true that $\lvert A\rvert\leq N/3+O(1)$?
```

### Problem 14

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/14
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/14.lean
- OEIS: ["A143824", "possible"]
- Last edited: 14 September 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$. Let $B\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be the set of integers which are representable in exactly one way as the sum of two elements from $A$.<br/><br/>Is it true that for all $\epsilon>0$ and large $N$\[\lvert \{1,\ldots,N\}\backslash B\rvert \gg_\epsilon N^{1/2-\epsilon}?\]Is it possible that\[\lvert \{1,\ldots,N\}\backslash B\rvert =o(N^{1/2})?\]
```

### Problem 17

### Problem 18

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/18
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/18.lean
- OEIS: ["A005153"]
- Last edited: 20 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
We call $m$ practical if every integer $1\leq n<m$ is the sum of distinct divisors of $m$. If $m$ is practical then let $h(m)$  be such that $h(m)$ many divisors always suffice.<br/><br/>Are there infinitely many practical $m$ such that\[h(m) < (\log\log m)^{O(1)}?\]Is it true that $h(n!)<n^{o(1)}$? Or perhaps even $h(n!)<(\log n)^{O(1)}$?
```

### Problem 20

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/20
- Status: open
- Prize: $1000
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/20.lean
- OEIS: ["A332077"]
- Last edited: 25 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(n,k)$ be minimal such that every family  $\mathcal{F}$  of $n$-uniform sets with $\lvert  \mathcal{F}\rvert \geq f(n,k)$ contains a $k$-sunflower. Is it true that\[f(n,k) < c_k^n\]for some constant $c_k>0$?
```

### Problem 21

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/21
- Status: proved
- Prize: $500
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A391599"]
- Last edited: 03 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(n)$ be minimal such that there is an intersecting family $\mathcal{F}$ of sets of size $n$ (so $A\cap B\neq\emptyset$ for all $A,B\in \mathcal{F}$) with $\lvert \mathcal{F}\rvert=f(n)$ such that any set $S$ with $\lvert S\rvert \leq n-1$ is disjoint from at least one $A\in \mathcal{F}$.<br/><br/>Is it true that\[f(n) \ll n?\]
```

### Problem 22

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/22

### Problem 27

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/27
- Status: disproved
- Prize: $100
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 06 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
An $\epsilon$-almost covering system is a set of congruences $a_i\pmod{n_i}$ for distinct moduli $n_1<\ldots<n_k$ such that the density of those integers which satisfy none of them is $\leq \epsilon$. <br/><br/>Is there a constant $C>1$ such that for every $\epsilon>0$ and $N\geq 1$ there is an $\epsilon$-almost covering system with $N\leq n_1<\cdots <n_k\leq CN$?
```

### Problem 32

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/32
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/32.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there a set $A\subset\mathbb{N}$ such that\[\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert = o((\log N)^2)\]and such that every large integer can be written as $p+a$ for some prime $p$ and $a\in A$? <br/><br/>Can the bound $O(\log N)$ be achieved? Must such an $A$ satisfy\[\liminf \frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{\log N}> 1?\]
```

### Problem 33

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/33
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/33.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subset\mathbb{N}$ be such that every large integer can be written as $n^2+a$ for some $a\in A$ and $n\geq 0$. What is the smallest possible value of\[\limsup \frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{N^{1/2}}?\]Is\[\liminf \frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{N^{1/2}}>1?\]
```

### Problem 34

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/34
- Status: disproved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/34.lean
- OEIS: ["A389241", "A234813", "A390187"]
- Last edited: 27 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For any permutation $\pi\in S_n$ of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ let $S(\pi)$ count the number of distinct consecutive sums, that is, sums of the shape $\sum_{u\leq i\leq v}\pi(i)$. Is it true that\[S(\pi) = o(n^2)\]for all $\pi\in S_n$?
```

### Problem 35

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/35
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $B\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ be an additive basis of order $k$ with $0\in B$. Is it true that for every $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ we have\[d_s(A+B)\geq \alpha+\frac{\alpha(1-\alpha)}{k},\]where $\alpha=d_s(A)$ and\[d_s(A) = \inf \frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{N}\]is the Schnirelmann density?
```

### Problem 36

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/36
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/36.lean
- OEIS: ["A393584", "possible"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Find the optimal constant $c>0$ such that the following holds. <br/><br/>For all sufficiently large $N$, if $A\sqcup B=\{1,\ldots,2N\}$ is a partition into two equal parts, so that $\lvert A\rvert=\lvert B\rvert=N$, then there is some $x$ such that the number of solutions to $a-b=x$ with $a\in A$ and $b\in B$ is at least $cN$.
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
We say that $A\subset \mathbb{N}$ is an essential component if $d_s(A+B)>d_s(B)$ for every $B\subset \mathbb{N}$ with $0<d_s(B)<1$ where $d_s$ is the <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schnirelmann_density" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">Schnirelmann density</a>.<br/><br/>Can a lacunary set $A\subset\mathbb{N}$ be an essential component?
```

### Problem 38

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/38
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/38.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 16 September 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Does there exist $B\subset\mathbb{N}$ which is not an additive basis, but is such that for every set $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ of Schnirelmann density $\alpha$ and every $N$ there exists $b\in B$ such that\[\lvert (A\cup (A+b))\cap \{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert\geq (\alpha+f(\alpha))N\]where $f(\alpha)>0$ for $0<\alpha <1 $?<br/><br/>The Schnirelmann density is defined by\[d_s(A) = \inf_{N\geq 1}\frac{\lvert A\cap\{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert}{N}.\]
```

### Problem 43

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/43
- Status: disproved
- Prize: $100
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/43.lean
- OEIS: ["A143824", "A227590", "A003022", "possible"]
- Last edited: 20 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $A,B\subset \{1,\ldots,N\}$ are two Sidon sets such that $(A-A)\cap(B-B)=\{0\}$ then is it true that\[ \binom{\lvert A\rvert}{2}+\binom{\lvert B\rvert}{2}\leq\binom{f(N)}{2}+O(1),\]where $f(N)$ is the maximum possible size of a Sidon set in $\{1,\ldots,N\}$? If $\lvert A\rvert=\lvert B\rvert$ then can this bound be improved to\[\binom{\lvert A\rvert}{2}+\binom{\lvert B\rvert}{2}\leq (1-c+o(1))\binom{f(N)}{2}\]for some constant $c>0$?
```

### Problem 54

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/54
- Status: solved
- Prize: $100
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 28 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
A set of integers $A$ is Ramsey $2$-complete if, whenever $A$ is $2$-coloured, all sufficiently large integers can be written as a monochromatic sum of elements of $A$. <br/><br/>Burr and Erdős <a href="#cite-BuEr85-54" onclick="event.preventDefault();addNewBox('BuEr85','54'); return false;">[BuEr85]</a> showed that there exists a constant $c>0$ such that it cannot be true that\[\lvert A\cap \{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert \leq c(\log N)^2\]for all large $N$ and that there exists a Ramsey $2$-complete $A$ such that for all large $N$\[\lvert A\cap \{1,\ldots,N\}\rvert <  (2\log_2N)^3.\]Improve either of these bounds.
```

### Problem 83

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/83
- Status: proved
- Prize: $500
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A071799", "A387635"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Suppose that we have a family $\mathcal{F}$ of subsets of $[4n]$ such that $\lvert A\rvert=2n$ for all $A\in\mathcal{F}$ and for every $A,B\in \mathcal{F}$ we have $\lvert A\cap B\rvert \geq 2$. Then\[\lvert \mathcal{F}\rvert \leq \frac{1}{2}\left(\binom{4n}{2n}-\binom{2n}{n}^2\right).\]
```

### Problem 84

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/84
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
The cycle set of a graph $G$ on $n$ vertices is a set $A\subseteq \{3,\ldots,n\}$ such that there is a cycle in $G$ of length $\ell$ if and only if $\ell \in A$. Let $f(n)$ count the number of possible such $A$. <br/><br/>Prove that $f(n)=o(2^n)$.<br/><br/>Prove that $f(n)/2^{n/2}\to \infty$.
```

Let $f(n)$ be maximal such that if $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ has $\lvert A\rvert=n$ then $\prod_{a\neq b\in A}(a+b)$ has at least $f(n)$ distinct prime factors. Is it true that $f(n)/\log n\to\infty$?
```

```

### Problem 129

- Status: open

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

In particular, can the chromatic number be infinite?

A finite set $A\subset \mathbb{R}^n$ is called Ramsey if, for any $k\geq 1$, there exists some $d=d(A,k)$ such that in any $k$-colouring of $\mathbb{R}^d$ there exists a monochromatic copy of $A$. Characterise the Ramsey sets in $\mathbb{R}^n$.
```

- Last edited: 28 December 2025

```text

Is it true that for any $c>0$ there exists some $C>1$ such that\[N(k,ck)\leq C^k?\]What about\[N(k,2)\leq C^k\]or\[N(k,\sqrt{k})\leq C^k?\]

### Problem 206

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/206
- Status: disproved (Lean)

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/206.lean

```text
Let $x>0$ be a real number. For any $n\geq 1$ let\[R_n(x) = \sum_{i=1}^n\frac{1}{m_i}<x\]be the maximal sum of $n$ distinct unit fractions which is $<x$.<br/><br/>Is it true that, for almost all $x$, for sufficiently large $n$, we have\[R_{n+1}(x)=R_n(x)+\frac{1}{m},\]where $m$ is minimal such that $m$ does not appear in $R_n(x)$ and the right-hand side is $<x$? (That is, are the best underapproximations eventually always constructed in a 'greedy' fashion?)
```

Let $n\geq 1$ and\[A=\{a_1<\cdots <a_{\phi(n)}\}=\{ 1\leq m<n : (m,n)=1\}.\]Is it true that\[ \sum_{1\leq k<\phi(n)}(a_{k+1}-a_k)^2 \ll \frac{n^2}{\phi(n)}?\]
```

### Problem 232

In particular, is $m_1\leq 1/4$?
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $a_n$ be a sequence of positive integers such that for every bounded sequence of integers $b_n$ (with $a_n+b_n\neq 0$ and $b_n\neq 0$ for all $n$) the sum\[\sum \frac{1}{a_n+b_n}\]is irrational. Are $a_n=2^n$ or $a_n=n!$ examples of such a sequence?
```

### Problem 280

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $n_1<n_2<\cdots $ be an infinite sequence of integers with associated $a_k\pmod{n_k}$, such that for some $\epsilon>0$ we have $n_k>(1+\epsilon)k\log k$ for all $k$. Then\[\#\{ m<n_k : m\not\equiv a_i\pmod{n_i} \textrm{ for }1\leq i\leq k\}\neq o(k).\]
```

### Problem 281

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/281
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/281.lean

- Last edited: 18 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $n_1<n_2<\cdots$ be an infinite sequence such that, for any choice of congruence classes $a_i\pmod{n_i}$, the set of integers not satisfying any of the congruences $a_i\pmod{n_i}$ has density $0$. <br/><br/>Is it true that for every $\epsilon>0$ there exists some $k$ such that, for every choice of congruence classes $a_i$, the density of integers not satisfying any of the congruences $a_i\pmod{n_i}$ for $1\leq i\leq k$ is less than $\epsilon$?
```

### Problem 282

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/282
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/282.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be an infinite set and consider the following greedy algorithm for a rational $x\in (0,1)$: choose the minimal $n\in A$ such that $n\geq 1/x$ and repeat with $x$ replaced by $x-\frac{1}{n}$. If this terminates after finitely many steps then this produces a representation of $x$ as the sum of distinct unit fractions with denominators from $A$.<br/><br/>Does this process always terminate if $x$ has odd denominator and $A$ is the set of odd numbers? More generally, for which pairs $x$ and $A$ does this process terminate?
```

### Problem 283

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/283
- Status: proved (Lean)

- Last edited: 28 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $p:\mathbb{Z}\to \mathbb{Z}$ be a polynomial whose leading coefficient is positive and such that there exists no $d\geq 2$ with $d\mid p(n)$ for all $n\geq 1$. Is it true that, for all sufficiently large $m$, there exist integers $1\leq n_1<\cdots <n_k$ such that\[1=\frac{1}{n_1}+\cdots+\frac{1}{n_k}\]and\[m=p(n_1)+\cdots+p(n_k)?\]
```

```

For integers $1\leq a<b$ let $D(a,b)$ be the minimal value of $n_k$ such that there exist integers $1\leq n_1<\cdots <n_k$ with\[\frac{a}{b}=\frac{1}{n_1}+\cdots+\frac{1}{n_k}.\]Estimate $D(b)=\max_{1\leq a<b}D(a,b)$. Is it true that\[D(b) \ll b(\log b)^{1+o(1)}?\]
```

- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/347.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 22 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there a sequence $A=\{a_1\leq a_2\leq \cdots\}$ of integers with\[\lim \frac{a_{n+1}}{a_n}=2\]such that\[P(A')= \left\{\sum_{n\in B}n : B\subseteq A'\textrm{ finite }\right\}\]has density $1$ for every cofinite subsequence $A'$ of $A$?
```

### Problem 348

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/348
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/348.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For what values of $0\leq m<n$ is there a complete sequence $A=\{a_1\leq a_2\leq \cdots\}$ of integers such that<ul>
<li> $A$ remains complete after removing any $m$ elements, but </li>
<li> $A$ is not complete after removing any $n$ elements? </li>
</ul>
```

### Problem 349

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/349
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/349.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For what values of $t,\alpha \in (0,\infty)$ is the sequence $\lfloor t\alpha^n\rfloor$ complete (that is, all sufficiently large integers are the sum of distinct integers of the form $\lfloor t\alpha^n\rfloor$)?
```

### Problem 350

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/350
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/350.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $A\subset\mathbb{N}$ is a finite set of integers which is dissociated (that is, all of the subset sums are distinct) then\[\sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{n}<2.\]
```

### Problem 351

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/351
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/351.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 02 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $p(x)\in \mathbb{Q}[x]$. Is it true that\[A=\{ p(n)+1/n : n\in \mathbb{N}\}\]is strongly complete, in the sense that, for any finite set $B$,\[\left\{\sum_{n\in X}n : X\subseteq A\backslash B\textrm{ finite }\right\}\]contains all sufficiently large integers?<br/>
```

### Problem 352

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/352
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/352.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there some $c>0$ such that every measurable $A\subseteq \mathbb{R}^2$ of measure $\geq c$ contains the vertices of a triangle of area 1?
```

### Problem 353

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/353
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/353.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{R}^2$ be a measurable set with infinite measure. Must $A$ contain the vertices of an isosceles trapezoid of area $1$? What about an isosceles triangle, or a right-angled triangle, or a cyclic quadrilateral, or a polygon with congruent sides?
```

### Problem 354

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/354
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/354.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 01 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\alpha,\beta\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}$ such that $\alpha/\beta$ is irrational. Is the multiset\[\{ \lfloor \alpha\rfloor,\lfloor 2\alpha\rfloor,\lfloor 4\alpha\rfloor,\ldots\}\cup \{ \lfloor \beta\rfloor,\lfloor 2\beta\rfloor,\lfloor 4\beta\rfloor,\ldots\}\]complete? That is, can all sufficiently large natural numbers $n$ be written as\[n=\sum_{s\in S}\lfloor 2^s\alpha\rfloor+\sum_{t\in T}\lfloor 2^t\beta\rfloor\]for some finite $S,T\subset \mathbb{N}$?<br/><br/>What if $2$ is replaced by some $\gamma\in(1,2)$?
```

### Problem 355

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/355
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/355.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 18 November 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there a lacunary sequence $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ (so that $A=\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}$ and there exists some $\lambda>1$ such that $a_{n+1}/a_n\geq \lambda$ for all $n\geq 1$) such that\[\left\{ \sum_{a\in A'}\frac{1}{a} : A'\subseteq A\textrm{ finite}\right\}\]contains all rationals in some open interval?
```

### Problem 356

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/356
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 16 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there some $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, there exist integers $a_1<\cdots<a_k\leq n$ such that there are at least $cn^2$ distinct integers of the form $\sum_{u\leq i\leq v}a_i$?
```

### Problem 357

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/357
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/357.lean
- OEIS: ["A364132", "A364153", "possible"]
- Last edited: 12 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $1\leq a_1<\cdots <a_k\leq n$ be integers such that all sums of the shape $\sum_{u\leq i\leq v}a_i$ are distinct. Let $f(n)$ be the maximal such $k$.<br/><br/>How does $f(n)$ grow? Is $f(n)=o(n)$?
```

### Problem 358

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/358
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/358.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 01 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A=\{a_1<\cdots\}$ be an infinite sequence of integers. Let $f(n)$ count the number of solutions to\[n=\sum_{u\leq i\leq v}a_i.\]Is there such an $A$ for which $f(n)\to \infty$ as $n\to \infty$? Or even where $f(n)\geq 2$ for all large $n$?<br/><br/>
```

### Problem 359

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/359
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/359.lean
- OEIS: ["A002048"]
- Last edited: 28 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $a_1<a_2<\cdots$ be an infinite sequence of integers such that $a_1=n$ and $a_{i+1}$ is the least integer which is not a sum of consecutive earlier $a_j$s. What can be said about the density of this sequence?<br/><br/>In particular, in the case $n=1$, can one prove that $a_k/k\to \infty$ and $a_k/k^{1+c}\to 0$ for any $c>0$?
```

### Problem 360

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/360
- Status: solved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(n)$ be minimal such that $\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$ can be partitioned into $f(n)$ classes so that $n$ cannot be expressed as a sum of distinct elements from the same class. How fast does $f(n)$ grow?
```

### Problem 361

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/361
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/361.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 17 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $c>0$ and $n$ be some large integer. What is the size of the largest $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,\lfloor cn\rfloor\}$ such that $n$ is not a sum of a subset of $A$? Does this depend on $n$ in an irregular way?
```

### Problem 362

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/362
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 27 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be a finite set of size $N$. Is it true that, for any fixed $t$, there are\[\ll \frac{2^N}{N^{3/2}}\]many $S\subseteq A$ such that $\sum_{n\in S}n=t$?<br/><br/>If we further ask that $\lvert S\rvert=l$ (for any fixed $l$) then is the number of solutions\[\ll \frac{2^N}{N^2},\]with the implied constant independent of $l$ and $t$?
```

```text
Is it true that there are only finitely many collections of disjoint intervals $I_1,\ldots,I_n$ of size $\lvert I_i\rvert \geq 4$ for $1\leq i\leq n$ such that\[\prod_{1\leq i\leq n}\prod_{m\in I_i}m\]is a square?<br/>
```

Are there any triples of consecutive positive integers all of which are powerful (i.e. if $p\mid n$ then $p^2\mid n$)?
```

### Problem 365

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/365
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A060355", "A060859", "A175155"]
- Last edited: 31 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Do all pairs of consecutive <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powerful_number" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">powerful numbers</a> $n$ and $n+1$ come from solutions to <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pell%27s_equation" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">Pell equations</a>? In other words, must either $n$ or $n+1$ be a square?<br/><br/>Is the number of such $n\leq x$ bounded by $(\log x)^{O(1)}$?

### Problem 366

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/366
- Status: verifiable

- OEIS: ["A060355"]
- Last edited: 20 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Are there any 2-full $n$ such that $n+1$ is 3-full? That is, if $p\mid n$ then $p^2\mid n$ and if $p\mid n+1$ then $p^3\mid n+1$.
```

### Problem 367

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/367
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A057521"]
- Last edited: 06 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $B_2(n)$ be the 2-full part of $n$ (that is, $B_2(n)=n/n'$ where $n'$ is the product of all primes that divide $n$ exactly once). Is it true that, for every fixed $k\geq 1$,\[\prod_{n\leq m<n+k}B_2(m) \ll n^{2+o(1)}?\]Or perhaps even $\ll_k n^2$?
```

### Problem 374

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/374
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A388851", "A387184", "A389117", "A389148"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For any $m\in \mathbb{N}$, let $F(m)$ be the minimal $k\geq 2$ (if it exists) such that there are $a_1<\cdots <a_k=m$ with $a_1!\cdots a_k!$ a square. Let $D_k=\{ m : F(m)=k\}$. What is the order of growth of $\lvert D_k\cap\{1,\ldots,n\}\rvert$ for $3\leq k\leq 6$? For example, is it true that $\lvert D_6\cap \{1,\ldots,n\}\rvert \gg n$?
```

### Problem 379

### Problem 380

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/380
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A070003", "A388654", "A387054", "A389100"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
We call an interval $[u,v]$ 'bad' if the greatest prime factor of $\prod_{u\leq m\leq v}m$ occurs with an exponent greater than $1$. Let $B(x)$ count the number of $n\leq x$ which are contained in at least one bad interval. Is it true that\[B(x)\sim \#\{ n\leq x: P(n)^2\mid n\},\]where $P(n)$ is the largest prime factor of $n$?
```

```

- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 31 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $k\leq n$. What choice of $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,n\}$ (with $\mathrm{gcd}(A)=1$) of size $\lvert A\rvert=k$ maximises the number of integers not representable as the sum of finitely many elements from $A$ (with repetitions allowed)? Is it $\{n,n-1,\ldots,n-k+1\}$?
```

Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$ with $n\neq p^k$ for any prime $p$ and $k\geq 0$. What is the largest integer not of the form\[\sum_{1\leq i<n}c_i\binom{n}{i}\]where the $c_i\geq 0$ are integers?

### Problem 436

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/436
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A000445", "possible"]
- Last edited: 25 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $p$ is a prime and $k,m\geq 2$ then let $r(k,m,p)$ be the minimal $r$ such that $r,r+1,\ldots,r+m-1$ are all $k$th power residues modulo $p$. Let\[\Lambda(k,m)=\limsup_{p\to \infty} r(k,m,p).\]Is it true that $\Lambda(k,2)$ is finite for all $k$? Is $\Lambda(k,3)$ finite for all odd $k$? How large are they?
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

Does\[\sum_{0<a_i< n}\frac{1}{a_i}\to \infty\]as $n\to \infty$? What about if we restrict the sum to those $i$ such that $n-a_j$ is divisible by some prime $\leq a_j$, or the complement of such $i$?
```

### Problem 461

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/461
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 28 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $s_t(n)$ be the $t$-smooth component of $n$ - that is, the product of all primes $p$ (with multiplicity) dividing $n$ such that $p<t$. Let $f(n,t)$ count the number of distinct possible values for $s_t(m)$ for $m\in [n+1,n+t]$. Is it true that\[f(n,t)\gg t\](uniformly, for all $t$ and $n$)?
```

### Problem 462

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/462
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A032742", "possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $p(n)$ denote the least prime factor of $n$. There is a constant $c>0$ such that\[\sum_{\substack{n<x\\ n\textrm{ not prime}}}\frac{p(n)}{n}\sim c\frac{x^{1/2}}{(\log x)^2}.\]Is it true that there exists a constant $C>0$ such that\[\sum_{x\leq n\leq x+Cx^{1/2}(\log x)^2}\frac{p(n)}{n} \gg 1\]for all large $x$?
```

- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/477.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 29 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there a polynomial $f:\mathbb{Z}\to \mathbb{Z}$ of degree at least $2$ and a set $A\subset \mathbb{Z}$ such that for any $n\in \mathbb{Z}$ there is exactly one $a\in A$ and $b\in \{ f(n) : n\in\mathbb{Z}\}$ such that $n=a+b$?
```

### Problem 478

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/478
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A210184"]
- Last edited: 04 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $p$ be a prime and\[A_p = \{ k! \pmod{p} : 1\leq k<p\}.\]Is it true that\[\lvert A_p\rvert \sim (1-\tfrac{1}{e})p?\]
```

```text
Is it true that, for all $k\neq 1$, there are infinitely many $n$ such that $2^n\equiv k\pmod{n}$?
```

### Problem 480

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/480
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/480.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 28 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $x_1,x_2,\ldots\in [0,1]$ be an infinite sequence. Is it true that\[\inf_n \liminf_{m\to \infty} n \lvert x_{m+n}-x_m\rvert\leq 5^{-1/2}\approx 0.447?\]
```

### Problem 481

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/481
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 03 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $a_1,\ldots,a_r,b_1,\ldots,b_r\in \mathbb{N}$ such that $\sum_{i}\frac{1}{a_i}>1$. For any finite sequence of $n$ (not necessarily distinct) integers $A=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ let $T(A)$ denote the sequence of length $rn$ given by\[(a_ix_j+b_i)_{1\leq j\leq n, 1\leq i\leq r}.\]Prove that, if $A_1=(1)$ and $A_{i+1}=T(A_i)$, then there must be some $A_k$ with repeated elements.
```

### Problem 482

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/482
- Status: solved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A004539"]
- Last edited: 28 September 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Define a sequence by $a_1=1$ and\[a_{n+1}=\lfloor\sqrt{2}(a_n+1/2)\rfloor\]for $n\geq 1$. The difference $a_{2n+1}-2a_{2n-1}$ is the $n$th digit in the binary expansion of $\sqrt{2}$.<br/><br/>Find similar results for $\theta=\sqrt{m}$, and other algebraic numbers.
```

### Problem 483

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/483
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A030126"]
- Last edited: 14 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(k)$ be the minimal $N$ such that if $\{1,\ldots,N\}$ is $k$-coloured then there is a monochromatic solution to $a+b=c$. Estimate $f(k)$. In particular, is it true that $f(k) < c^k$ for some constant $c>0$?
```

### Problem 484

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/484
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Prove that there exists an absolute constant $c>0$ such that, whenever $\{1,\ldots,N\}$ is $k$-coloured (and $N$ is large enough depending on $k$) then there are at least $cN$ many integers in $\{1,\ldots,N\}$ which are representable as a monochromatic sum (that is, $a+b$ where $a,b\in \{1,\ldots,N\}$ are in the same colour class and $a\neq b$).
```

### Problem 485

- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures

- Last edited: 29 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(k)$ be the minimum number of terms in $P(x)^2$, where $P\in \mathbb{Q}[x]$ ranges over all polynomials with exactly $k$ non-zero terms. Is it true that $f(k)\to\infty$ as $k\to \infty$?
```

### Problem 486

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/486
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/486.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 12 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$, and for each $n\in A$ choose some $X_n\subseteq \mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}$. Let\[B = \{ m\in \mathbb{N} : m\not\in X_n\pmod{n}\textrm{ for all }n\in A\textrm{ with }m>n\}.\]Must $B$ have a logarithmic density, i.e. is it true that\[\lim_{x\to \infty} \frac{1}{\log x}\sum_{\substack{m\in B\\ m<x}}\frac{1}{m}\]exists?
```

### Problem 487

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ have positive density. Must there exist distinct $a,b,c\in A$ such that $[a,b]=c$ (where $[a,b]$ is the least common multiple of $a$ and $b$)?
```

### Problem 488

- Last edited: 31 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A$ be a finite set and\[B=\{ n \geq 1 : a\mid n\textrm{ for some }a\in A\}.\]Is it true that, for every $m>n\geq \max(A)$,\[\frac{\lvert B\cap [1,m]\rvert }{m}< 2\frac{\lvert B\cap [1,n]\rvert}{n}?\]
```

### Problem 489

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/489
- Status: open

- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be a set such that $\lvert A\cap [1,x]\rvert=o(x^{1/2})$. Let\[B=\{ n\geq 1 : a\nmid n\textrm{ for all }a\in A\}.\]If $B=\{b_1<b_2<\cdots\}$ then is it true that\[\lim \frac{1}{x}\sum_{b_i<x}(b_{i+1}-b_i)^2\]exists (and is finite)?
```

### Problem 490

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A,B\subseteq \{1,\ldots,N\}$ be such that all the products $ab$ with $a\in A$ and $b\in B$ are distinct. Is it true that\[\lvert A\rvert \lvert B\rvert \ll \frac{N^2}{\log N}?\]
```

### Problem 491

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/491
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 30 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f:\mathbb{N}\to \mathbb{R}$ be an additive function (i.e. $f(ab)=f(a)+f(b)$ whenever $(a,b)=1$). If there is a constant $c$ such that $\lvert f(n+1)-f(n)\rvert <c$ for all $n$ then must there exist some $c'$ such that\[f(n)=c'\log n+O(1)?\]
```

### Problem 492

- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

```

```text

Is it true that the number of sums of the shape\[\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_iz_i \textrm{ for }\epsilon_i\in \{-1,1\}\]which lie in $D$ is at most $\binom{n}{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}$?

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f$ be a Rademacher multiplicative function: a random $\{-1,0,1\}$-valued multiplicative function, where for each prime $p$ we independently choose $f(p)\in \{-1,1\}$ uniformly at random, and for square-free integers $n$ we extend $f(p_1\cdots p_r)=f(p_1)\cdots f(p_r)$ (and $f(n)=0$ if $n$ is not squarefree). Does there exist some constant $c>0$ such that, almost surely,\[\limsup_{N\to \infty}\frac{\sum_{m\leq N}f(m)}{\sqrt{N\log\log N}}=c?\]
```

### Problem 521

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/521
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 19 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $(\epsilon_k)_{k\geq 0}$ be independently uniformly chosen at random from $\{-1,1\}$. If $R_n$ counts the number of real roots of $f_n(z)=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n}\epsilon_k z^k$ then is it true that, almost surely,\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{R_n}{\log n}=\frac{2}{\pi}?\]
```

- Last edited: 06 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(z)=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n} \epsilon_k z^k$ be a random polynomial, where $\epsilon_k\in \{-1,1\}$ independently uniformly at random for $0\leq k\leq n$. <br/><br/>Is it true that, if $R_n$ is the number of roots of $f(z)$ in $\{ z\in \mathbb{C} : \lvert z\rvert \leq 1\}$, then\[\frac{R_n}{n/2}\to 1\]almost surely?
```

### Problem 523

- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(z)=\sum_{0\leq k\leq n} \epsilon_k z^k$ be a random polynomial, where $\epsilon_k\in \{-1,1\}$ independently uniformly at random for $0\leq k\leq n$. <br/><br/>Does there exist some constant $C>0$ such that, almost surely,\[\max_{\lvert z\rvert=1}\left\lvert \sum_{k\leq n}\epsilon_k(t)z^k\right\rvert=(C+o(1))\sqrt{n\log n}?\]
```

### Problem 524

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/524

```text

```

### Problem 526

### Problem 527

- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 27 December 2025

```text
Let $d_k(n)$ be the expected distance from the origin after taking $n$ random steps from the origin in $\mathbb{Z}^k$ (conditional on no self intersections) - that is, a <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-avoiding_walk" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">self-avoiding walk</a>. Is it true that\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{d_2(n)}{n^{1/2}}= \infty?\]Is it true that\[d_k(n)\ll n^{1/2}\]for $k\geq 3$?

- OEIS: ["A143824", "possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/564
- Status: open
- Prize: $500
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/564.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 18 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $R_3(n)$ be the minimal $m$ such that if the edges of the $3$-uniform hypergraph on $m$ vertices are $2$-coloured then there is a monochromatic copy of the complete $3$-uniform hypergraph on $n$ vertices.<br/><br/>Is there some constant $c>0$ such that\[R_3(n) \geq 2^{2^{cn}}?\]
```

### Problem 565

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/565
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 18 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $R^*(G)$ be the induced Ramsey number: the minimal $m$ such that there is a graph $H$ on $m$ vertices such that any $2$-colouring of the edges of $H$ contains an induced monochromatic copy of $G$.<br/><br/>Is it true that\[R^*(G) \leq 2^{O(n)}\]for any graph $G$ on $n$ vertices?
```

### Problem 566

- Last edited: 18 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $G$ be such that any subgraph on $k$ vertices has at most $2k-3$ edges. Is it true that, if $H$ has $m$ edges and no isolated vertices, then\[R(G,H)\ll m?\]
```

```text

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/568

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

```text

```

### Problem 575

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/575
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 18 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $\mathcal{F}$ is a finite set of finite graphs then $\mathrm{ex}(n;\mathcal{F})$ is the maximum number of edges a graph on $n$ vertices can have without containing any subgraphs from $\mathcal{F}$. Note that it is trivial that $\mathrm{ex}(n;\mathcal{F})\leq \mathrm{ex}(n;G)$ for every $G\in\mathcal{F}$. <br/><br/>Is it true that, for every $\mathcal{F}$, if there is a bipartite graph in $\mathcal{F}$ then there exists some bipartite $G\in\mathcal{F}$ such that\[\mathrm{ex}(n;G)\ll_{\mathcal{F}}\mathrm{ex}(n;\mathcal{F})?\]
```

### Problem 600

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/600
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/600.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $e(n,r)$ be minimal such that every graph on $n$ vertices with at least $e(n,r)$ edges, each edge contained in at least one triangle, must have an edge contained in at least $r$ triangles. Let $r\geq 2$. Is it true that\[e(n,r+1)-e(n,r)\to \infty\]as $n\to \infty$? Is it true that\[\frac{e(n,r+1)}{e(n,r)}\to 1\]as $n\to \infty$?
```

### Problem 610

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/610
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 02 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For a graph $G$ let $\tau(G)$ denote the minimal number of vertices that include at least one from each maximal clique of $G$ (sometimes called the clique transversal number).<br/><br/>Estimate $\tau(G)$. In particular, is it true that if $G$ has $n$ vertices then\[\tau(G) \leq n-\omega(n)\sqrt{n}\]for some $\omega(n)\to \infty$, or even\[\tau(G) \leq n-c\sqrt{n\log n}\]for some absolute constant $c>0$?
```

### Problem 611

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/611
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $G$ be a connected graph with $n$ vertices, minimum degree $d$, and diameter $D$. Show if that $G$ contains no $K_{2r}$ and $(r-1)(3r+2)\mid d$ then\[D\leq \frac{2(r-1)(3r+2)}{2r^2-1}\frac{n}{d}+O(1),\]and if $G$ contains no $K_{2r+1}$ and $3r-1 \mid d$ then\[D\leq \frac{3r-1}{r}\frac{n}{d}+O(1).\]
```

### Problem 624

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/624

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $X$ be a finite set of size $n$ and $H(n)$ be such that there is a function $f:\{A : A\subseteq X\}\to X$ so that for every $Y\subseteq X$ with $\lvert Y\rvert \geq H(n)$ we have\[\{ f(A) : A\subseteq Y\}=X.\]Prove that\[H(n)-\log_2 n \to \infty.\]
```

### Problem 625

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/625
- Status: open
- Prize: $1000
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
The cochromatic number of $G$, denoted by $\zeta(G)$, is the minimum number of colours needed to colour the vertices of $G$ such that each colour class induces either a complete graph or empty graph. Let $\chi(G)$ denote the chromatic number.<br/><br/>If $G$ is a random graph with $n$ vertices and each edge included independently with probability $1/2$ then is it true that almost surely\[\chi(G) - \zeta(G) \to \infty\]as $n\to \infty$?
```

### Problem 626

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/626
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $k\geq 4$ and $g_k(n)$ denote the largest $m$ such that there is a graph on $n$ vertices with chromatic number $k$ and girth $>m$ (i.e. contains no cycle of length $\leq m$). Does\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{g_k(n)}{\log n}\]exist?<br/><br/>Conversely, if $h^{(m)}(n)$ is the maximal chromatic number of a graph on $n$ vertices with girth $>m$ then does\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\log h^{(m)}(n)}{\log n}\]exist, and what is its value?
```

### Problem 627

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/627

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

```

Is it true that if $A\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ is a set of $n$ points such that every subset of $3$ points determines $3$ distinct distances (i.e. $A$ has no isosceles triangles) then $A$ must determine at least $f(n)n$ distinct distances, for some $f(n)\to \infty$?
```

### Problem 662

- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

For example $f(1)=6$, $f(\sqrt{3})=12$, and $f(3)=18$.<br/><br/>Let $x_1,\ldots,x_n\in \mathbb{R}^2$ be such that $d(x_i,x_j)\geq 1$ for all $i\neq j$. Is it true that, provided $n$ is sufficiently large depending on $t$, the number of distances $d(x_i,x_j)\leq t$ is less than or equal to $f(t)$ with equality perhaps only for the triangular lattice?<br/><br/>In particular, is it true that the number of distances $\leq \sqrt{3}-\epsilon$ is less than $1$?
```

### Problem 671

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/671
- Status: open
- Prize: $250
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Given $a_{i}^n\in [-1,1]$ for all $1\leq i\leq n<\infty$ we define $p_{i}^n$ as the unique polynomial of degree $n-1$ such that $p_{i}^n(a_{i}^n)=1$ and $p_{i}^n(a_{i'}^n)=0$ if $1\leq i'\leq n$ with $i\neq i'$. We similarly define\[\mathcal{L}^nf(x) = \sum_{1\leq i\leq n}f(a_i^n)p_i^n(x),\]the unique polynomial of degree $n-1$ which agrees with $f$ on $a_i^n$ for $1\leq i\leq n$ (that is, the sequence of Lagrange interpolation polynomials).<br/><br/>Is there such a sequence of $a_i^n$ such that for every continuous $f:[-1,1]\to \mathbb{R}$ there exists some $x\in [-1,1]$ where\[\limsup_{n\to \infty} \sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\lvert p_{i}^n(x)\rvert=\infty\]and yet\[\mathcal{L}^nf(x) \to f(x)?\]Is there such a sequence such that\[\limsup_{n\to \infty} \sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\lvert p_{i}^n(x)\rvert=\infty\]for every $x\in [-1,1]$ and yet for every continuous $f:[-1,1]\to \mathbb{R}$ there exists $x\in [-1,1]$ with\[\mathcal{L}^nf(x) \to f(x)?\]
```

### Problem 675

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/675
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
We say that $A\subset \mathbb{N}$ has the translation property if, for every $n$, there exists some integer $t_n\geq 1$ such that, for all $1\leq a\leq n$,\[a\in A\quad\textrm{ if and only if }\quad a+t_n\in A.\]<ul>
<li>Does the set of the sums of two squares have the translation property?</li>
<li>If we partition all primes into $P\sqcup Q$, such that each set contains $\gg x/\log x$ many primes $\leq x$ for all large $x$, then can the set of integers only divisible by primes from $P$ have the translation property?</li>
<li>If $A$ is the set of squarefree numbers then how fast does the minimal such $t_n$ grow? Is it true that $t_n>\exp(n^c)$ for some constant $c>0$?</li>
</ul>
```

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Is there some $h(n)\to \infty$ such that for all $2\leq i<j\leq n/2$\[\textrm{gcd}\left( \binom{n}{i},\binom{n}{j}\right) \geq h(n)?\]
```

### Problem 699

Is it true that for every $1\leq i<j\leq n/2$ there exists some prime $p\geq i$ such that\[p\mid \textrm{gcd}\left(\binom{n}{i}, \binom{n}{j}\right)?\]
```

### Problem 700

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/700
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/700.lean
- OEIS: ["A091963", "possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let\[f(n)=\min_{1<k\leq n/2}\textrm{gcd}\left(n,\binom{n}{k}\right).\]<ul>
<li>Characterise those composite $n$ such that $f(n)=n/P(n)$, where $P(n)$ is the largest prime dividing $n$.</li>
<li>Are there infinitely many composite $n$ such that $f(n)>n^{1/2}$?</li>
<li> Is it true that, for every composite $n$,\[f(n) \ll_A \frac{n}{(\log n)^A}\]for every $A>0$?</li>
</ul>
```

### Problem 701

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/701
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/701.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\mathcal{F}$ be a family of sets closed under taking subsets (i.e. if $B\subseteq A\in\mathcal{F}$ then $B\in \mathcal{F}$). There exists some element $x$ such that whenever $\mathcal{F}'\subseteq \mathcal{F}$ is an intersecting subfamily we have\[\lvert \mathcal{F}'\rvert \leq \lvert \{ A\in \mathcal{F} : x\in A\}\rvert.\]
```

### Problem 703

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/703
- Status: proved
- Prize: $250
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A390645"]
- Last edited: 16 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $r\geq 1$ and define $T(n,r)$ to be maximal such that there exists a family $\mathcal{F}$ of subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $T(n,r)$ such that $\lvert A\cap B\rvert\neq r$ for all $A,B\in \mathcal{F}$.<br/><br/>Estimate $T(n,r)$ for $r\geq 2$. In particular, is it true that for every $\epsilon>0$ there exists $\delta>0$ such that for all $\epsilon n<r<(1/2-\epsilon) n$ we have\[T(n,r)<(2-\delta)^n?\]
```

### Problem 704

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/704
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $G_n$ be the unit distance graph in $\mathbb{R}^n$, with two vertices joined by an edge if and only if the distance between them is $1$.<br/><br/>Estimate the chromatic number $\chi(G_n)$. Does it grow exponentially in $n$? Does\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\chi(G_n)^{1/n}\]exist?
```

### Problem 705

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/705

- Last edited: 27 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $G$ be a finite unit distance graph in $\mathbb{R}^2$ (i.e. the vertices are a finite collection of points in $\mathbb{R}^2$ and there is an edge between two points if and only if the distance between them is $1$).<br/><br/>Is there some $k$ such that if $G$ has girth $\geq k$ (i.e. $G$ contains no cycles of length $<k$) then $\chi(G)\leq 3$?
```

### Problem 706

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/706
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $L(r)$ be such that if $G$ is a graph formed by taking a finite set of points $P$ in $\mathbb{R}^2$ and some set $A\subset (0,\infty)$ of size $r$, where the vertex set is $P$ and there is an edge between two points if and only if their distance is a member of $A$, then $\chi(G)\leq L(r)$.<br/><br/>Estimate $L(r)$. In particular, is it true that $L(r)\leq r^{O(1)}$?
```

### Problem 708

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/708
- Status: open
- Prize: $100
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $g(n)$ be minimal such that for any $A\subseteq [2,\infty)\cap \mathbb{N}$ with $\lvert A\rvert =n$ and any set $I$ of $\max(A)$ consecutive integers there exists some $B\subseteq I$ with $\lvert B\rvert=g(n)$ such that\[\prod_{a\in A} a \mid \prod_{b\in B}b.\]Is it true that\[g(n) \leq (2+o(1))n?\]Or perhaps even $g(n)\leq 2n$?
```

- OEIS: ["possible"]

```

### Problem 712

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/712
- Status: open

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 05 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

```

### Problem 720

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/720
- Status: proved
- Prize: $100
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 26 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\hat{R}(G)$ denote the size Ramsey number, the minimal number of edges $m$ such that there is a graph $H$ with $m$ edges such that in any $2$-colouring of the edges of $H$ there is a monochromatic copy of $G$. <br/><br/>Is it true that, if $P_n$ is the path of length $n$, then\[\hat{R}(P_n)/n\to \infty\]and\[\hat{R}(P_n)/n^2 \to 0?\]Is it true that, if $C_n$ is the cycle with $n$ edges, then\[\hat{R}(C_n) =o(n^2)?\]
```

```text

Does there always exist a block $r-(n,k,1)$ design (or <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steiner_system" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">Steiner system</a> with parameters $(n,k,r)$), provided the trivial necessary divisibility conditions $\binom{k-i}{r-i}\mid \binom{n-i}{r-i}$ are satisfied for every $0\leq i<r$?<br/><br/>That is, can one find a family of $\binom{n}{k}\binom{k}{r}^{-1}$ many subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$, all of size $k$, such that any $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,n\}$ of size $r$ is contained in exactly one set in the family?
```

Let $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be such that $A+A$ has positive density. Can one always decompose $A=A_1\sqcup A_2$ such that $A_1+A_1$ and $A_2+A_2$ both have positive density?<br/><br/>Is there a basis $A$ of order $2$ such that if $A=A_1\sqcup A_2$ then $A_1+A_1$ and $A_2+A_2$ cannot both have bounded gaps?
```

### Problem 777

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/777

### Problem 778

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/778
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Alice and Bob play a game on the edges of $K_n$, alternating colouring edges by red (Alice) and blue (Bob). Alice goes first, and wins if at the end the largest red clique is larger than any of the blue cliques.<br/><br/>Does Bob have a winning strategy for $n\geq 3$? (Erdős believed the answer is yes.)<br/><br/>If we change the game so that Bob colours two edges after each edge that Alice colours, but now require Bob's largest clique to be strictly larger than Alice's, then does Bob have a winning strategy for $n>3$?<br/><br/>Finally, consider the game when Alice wins if the maximum degree of the red subgraph is larger than the maximum degree of the blue subgraph. Who wins?
```

- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A,B\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ be infinite sets such that $A+B$ contains all large integers. Let $A(x)=\lvert A\cap [1,x]\rvert$ and similarly for $B(x)$. Is it true that if $A(x)B(x)\sim x$ then\[A(x)B(x)-x\to \infty\]as $x\to \infty$?
```

### Problem 786

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/786
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/786.lean
- OEIS: ["A143301", "possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\epsilon>0$. Is there some set $A\subset \mathbb{N}$ of density $>1-\epsilon$ such that $a_1\cdots a_r=b_1\cdots b_s$ with $a_i,b_j\in A$ can only hold when $r=s$?<br/><br/>Similarly, can one always find a set $A\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}$ with this property of size $\geq (1-o(1))N$?
```

### Problem 787

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/787
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $g(n)$ be maximal such that given any set $A\subset \mathbb{R}$ with $\lvert A\rvert=n$ there exists some $B\subseteq A$ of size $\lvert B\rvert\geq g(n)$ such that $b_1+b_2\not\in A$ for all $b_1\neq b_2\in B$.<br/><br/>Estimate $g(n)$.
```

### Problem 788

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/788
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 26 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f(n)$ be maximal such that if $B\subset (2n,4n)\cap \mathbb{N}$ there exists some $C\subset (n,2n)\cap \mathbb{N}$ such that $c_1+c_2\not\in B$ for all $c_1\neq c_2\in C$ and $\lvert C\rvert+\lvert B\rvert \geq f(n)$. <br/><br/>Estimate $f(n)$. In particular is it true that $f(n)\leq n^{1/2+o(1)}$?
```

### Problem 789

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/789
- Status: open

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/789.lean

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $h(n)$ be maximal such that if $A\subseteq \mathbb{Z}$ with $\lvert A\rvert=n$ then there is $B\subseteq A$ with $\lvert B\rvert \geq h(n)$ such that if $a_1+\cdots+a_r=b_1+\cdots+b_s$ with $a_i,b_i\in B$ then $r=s$.<br/><br/>Estimate $h(n)$.
```

### Problem 790

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/790
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $l(n)$ be maximal such that if $A\subset\mathbb{Z}$ with $\lvert A\rvert=n$ then there exists a sum-free $B\subseteq A$ with $\lvert B\rvert \geq l(n)$ - that is, $B$ is such that there are no solutions to\[a_1=a_2+\cdots+a_r\]with $a_i\in B$ all distinct.<br/><br/>Estimate $l(n)$. In particular, is it true that $l(n)n^{-1/2}\to \infty$? Is it true that $l(n)< n^{1-c}$ for some $c>0$?
```

- OEIS: ["N/A"]

### Problem 807

- Status: disproved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
The bipartition number $\tau(G)$ of a graph $G$ is the smallest number of pairwise edge disjoint complete bipartite graphs whose union is $G$. The independence number $\alpha(G)$ is the size of the largest independent subset of $G$.<br/><br/>Is it true that, if $G$ is a random graph on $n$ vertices with edge probability $1/2$, then\[\tau(G)=n-\alpha(G)\]almost surely?
```

### Problem 811

- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 14 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

We say that an edge-colouring of $K_n$ using $m$ colours is balanced if every vertex sees exactly $\lfloor n/m\rfloor$ many edges of each colours. <br/><br/>For which graphs $G$ is it true that, if $m=e(G)$, for all large $n\equiv 1\pmod{m}$, every balanced edge-colouring of $K_n$ with $m$ colours contains a rainbow copy of $G$? (That is, a subgraph isomorphic to $G$ where each edge receives a different colour.)
```

Let $A\subset\mathbb{N}$ be the set of cubes. Is it true that\[1_A\ast 1_A(n) \ll (\log n)^{O(1)}?\]
```

### Problem 863

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/863
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

(That is, $A$ is a $B_2[r]$ set.)<br/><br/>Similarly, let $B\subseteq \{1,\ldots,N\}$ be a set of maximal size such that there are at most $r$ solutions to $n=a-b$ for any $n$. <br/><br/>If $\lvert A\rvert\sim c_rN^{1/2}$ as $N\to \infty$ and $\lvert B\rvert \sim c_r'N^{1/2}$ as $N\to \infty$ then is it true that $c_r\neq c_r'$ for $r\geq 2$? - Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/872.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Consider the two-player game in which players alternately choose integers from $\{2,3,\ldots,n\}$ to be included in some set $A$ (the same set for both players) such that no $a\mid b$ for $a\neq b\in A$. <br/><br/>The game ends when no legal move is possible. One player wants the game to last as long as possible, the other wants the game to end quickly. How long can the game be guaranteed to last for? <br/><br/>At least $\epsilon n$ moves? (For $\epsilon>0$ and $n$ sufficiently large.) At least $(1-\epsilon)\frac{n}{2}$ moves?
```

### Problem 873

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/873
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/873.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A=\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ and let $F(A,X,k)$ count the number of $i$ such that\[[a_i,a_{i+1},\ldots,a_{i+k-1}] < X,\]where the left-hand side is the least common multiple. Is it true that, for every $\epsilon >0$, there exists some $k$ such that\[F(A,X,k)<X^\epsilon?\]
```

### Problem 874

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/874
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $k(N)$ denote the size of the largest set $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,N\}$ such that the sets\[S_r = \{ a_1+\cdots +a_r : a_1<\cdots<a_r\in A\}\]are disjoint for distinct $r\geq 1$. Estimate $k(N)$ - in particular, is it true that $k(N)\sim 2N^{1/2}$?
```

### Problem 875

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/875
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A=\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}\subset \mathbb{N}$ be an infinite set such that the sets\[S_r = \{ a_1+\cdots +a_r : a_1<\cdots<a_r\in A\}\]are disjoint for distinct $r\geq 1$. How fast can such a sequence grow? How small can $a_{n+1}-a_n$ be? In particular, for which $c$ is it possible that $a_{n+1}-a_n\leq n^{c}$?
```

### Problem 876

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/876
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A=\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}\subset \mathbb{N}$ be an infinite sum-free set - that is, there are no solutions to\[a=b_1+\cdots+b_r\]with $b_1<\cdots<b_r<a\in A$. How small can $a_{n+1}-a_n$ be? Is it possible that $a_{n+1}-a_n<n$?
```

### Problem 877

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/877
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 02 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $f_m(n)$ count the number of maximal sum-free subsets $A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$ - that is, there are no solutions to $a=b+c$ in $A$ and $A$ is maximal with this property. Estimate $f(n)$ - is it true that $f_m(n)=o(2^{n/2})$?
```

### Problem 878

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/878
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A339378", "possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $n=\prod_{1\leq i\leq t} p_i^{k_i}$ is the factorisation of $n$ into distinct primes then let\[f(n)=\sum p_i^{\ell_i},\]where $\ell_i$ is chosen such that $n\in [p_i^{\ell_i},p_i^{\ell_i+1})$. Furthermore, let\[F(n)=\max \sum_{i=1}^t a_i\]where the maximum is taken over all $a_1,\ldots,a_t\leq n$ such that $(a_i,a_j)=1$ for $i\neq j$ and all prime factors of each $a_i$ are prime factors of $n$.<br/><br/>Is it true that, for almost all $n$,\[f(n)=o(n\log\log n)\]and\[F(n) \gg n\log\log n?\]Is it true that\[\max_{n\leq x}f(n)\sim \frac{x\log x}{\log\log x}?\]Is it true that (for all $x$, or perhaps just for all large $x$)\[\max_{n\leq x}f(n)=\max_{n\leq x}F(n)?\]Find an asymptotic formula for the number of $n<x$ such that $f(n)=F(n)$. Find an asymptotic formula for\[H(x)=\sum_{n<x}\frac{f(n)}{n}.\]Is it true that\[H(x) \ll x\log\log\log\log x?\]
```

### Problem 879

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/879
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A186736"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Call a set $S\subseteq \{1,\ldots,n\}$ admissible if $(a,b)=1$ for all $a\neq b\in S$. Let\[G(n) = \max_{S\subseteq \{1,\ldots,n\}} \sum_{a\in S}a\]and\[H(n)=\sum_{p<n}p+ n\pi(n^{1/2}).\]Is it true that\[G(n) >H(n)-n^{1+o(1)}?\]Is it true that, for every $k\geq 2$, if $n$ is sufficiently large then the admissible set which maximises $G(n)$ contains at least one integer with at least $k$ prime factors?
```

### Problem 883

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For $A\subseteq \{1,\ldots,n\}$ let $G(A)$ be the graph with vertex set $A$, where two integers are joined by an edge if they are coprime.<br/><br/>Is it true that if\[\lvert A\rvert >\lfloor\tfrac{n}{2}\rfloor+\lfloor\tfrac{n}{3}\rfloor-\lfloor\tfrac{n}{6}\rfloor\]then $G(A)$ contains all odd cycles of length $\leq \frac{n}{3}+1$?<br/><br/>Is it true that, for every $\ell\geq 1$, if $n$ is sufficiently large and\[\lvert A\rvert >\lfloor\tfrac{n}{2}\rfloor+\lfloor\tfrac{n}{3}\rfloor-\lfloor\tfrac{n}{6}\rfloor\]then $G(A)$ must contain a complete $(1,\ell,\ell)$ triparite graph on $2\ell+1$ vertices?
```

```

### Problem 956

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/956
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $C,D\subseteq \mathbb{R}^2$ then the distance between $C$ and $D$ is defined by\[\delta(C,D)=\inf_{\substack{c\in C\\ d\in D}}\| c-d\|.\]Let $h(n)$ be the maximal number of unit distances between disjoint convex translates. That is, the maximal $m$ such that there is a compact convex set $C\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ and a set $X$ of size $n$ such that all $(C+x)_{x\in X}$ are disjoint and there are $m$ pairs $x_1,x_2\in X$ such that\[\delta(C+x_1,C+x_2)=1.\]Determine $h(n)$ - in particular, prove that there exists a constant $c>0$ such that $h(n)>n^{1+c}$ for all large $n$.<br/>
```

### Problem 960

- Status: disproved

- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $r,k\geq 2$ be fixed. Let $A\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ be a set of $n$ points with no $k$ points on a line. Determine the threshold $f_{r,k}(n)$ such that if there are at least $f_{r,k}(n)$ many ordinary lines (lines containing exactly two points) then there is a set $A'\subseteq A$ of $r$ points such that all $\binom{r}{2}$ many lines determined by $A'$ are ordinary.<br/><br/>Is it true that $f_{r,k}(n)=o(n^2)$, or perhaps even $\ll n$?
```

- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/997.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Call $x_1,x_2,\ldots \in (0,1)$ well-distributed if, for every $\epsilon>0$, if $k$ is sufficiently large then, for all $n>0$ and intervals $I\subseteq [0,1]$,\[\lvert \# \{ n<m\leq n+k : x_m\in I\} - \lvert I\rvert k\rvert < \epsilon k.\]Is it true that, for every $\alpha$, the sequence $\{ \alpha p_n\}$ is not well-distributed, if $p_n$ is the sequence of primes?
```

### Problem 998

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/998
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 05 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\alpha$ be an irrational number. Is it true that if, for all large $n$,\[\#\{ 1\leq m\leq n : \{ \alpha m\} \in [u,v)\} = n(v-u)+O(1)\]then $u=\{\alpha k\}$ and $v=\{\alpha \ell\}$ for some integers $k$ and $\ell$?
```

### Problem 999

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/999
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For any function $f:\mathbb{N}\to \mathbb{N}$ the property that, for almost all $\alpha$\[\left\lvert \alpha-\frac{p}{q}\right\rvert < \frac{f(q)}{q}\]has infinitely many solutions with $(p,q)=1$, is equivalent to\[\sum_{q\geq 1}\phi(q)\frac{f(q)}{q}=\infty.\]
```

### Problem 1000

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1000
- Status: proved (Lean)
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 05 December 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $A=\{n_1<n_2<\cdots\}$ be an infinite sequence of integers, and let $\phi_A(k)$ count the number of $1\leq m\leq n_k$ such that the fraction $\frac{m}{n_k}$ does not have denominator $n_j$ for $j<k$ when written in lowest form; equivalently,\[\frac{n_k}{(m,n_k)}\neq n_j\]for all $1\leq j<k$.<br/><br/>Is there a sequence $A$ such that\[\lim_{N\to \infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\leq N}\frac{\phi_A(k)}{n_k}=0?\]
```

### Problem 1001

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1001
- Status: solved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $S(N,A,c)$ be the measure of the set of those $\alpha\in (0,1)$ such that\[\left\lvert \alpha-\frac{x}{y}\right\rvert< \frac{A}{y^2}\]for some $N\leq y\leq cN$ and $(x,y)=1$. Does\[\lim_{N\to \infty}S(N,A,c)=f(A,c)\]exist? What is its explicit form?
```

### Problem 1002

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1002
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/1002.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For any $0<\alpha<1$, let\[f(\alpha,n)=\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\{ \alpha k\}).\]Does $f(\alpha,n)$ have an asymptotic distribution function?<br/><br/>In other words, is there a non-decreasing function $g$ such that $g(-\infty)=0$, $g(\infty)=1$,<br/>and\[\lim_{n\to \infty}\lvert \{ \alpha\in (0,1): f(\alpha,n)\leq c\}\rvert=g(c)?\]
```

### Problem 1004

- Status: open
- Prize: no

```text

### Problem 1005

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1005
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["A386893"]

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $\frac{a_1}{b_1},\frac{a_2}{b_2},\ldots$ be the <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farey_sequence" rel="nofollow noopener noreferrer ugc" target="_blank">Farey fractions</a> of order $n\geq 4$. Let $f(n)$ be the largest integer such that if $1\leq k<l\leq k+f(n)$ then $\frac{a_k}{b_k}$ and $\frac{a_l}{b_l}$ are similarly ordered - in other words,\[(a_k-a_l)(b_k-b_l)\geq 0.\]Estimate $f(n)$ - in particular, is there a constant $c>0$ such that $f(n)=(c+o(1))n$ for all large $n$?
```

Let $F\subset\mathbb{C}$ be a closed set of transfinite diameter $1$ which is not contained in any closed disc of radius $1$. <br/><br/>If $f(z)=\prod_{i=1}^n(z-z_i)\in\mathbb{C}[x]$ with all $z_i\in F$ then can\[\{ z: \lvert f(z)\rvert < 1\}\]have $n$ connected components?<br/><br/>If the transfinite diameter of $F$ is $<1$ then must this set only have at most $(1-c)n$ connected components, where $c>0$ depends only on $F$ (or just the transfinite diameter of $F$)?
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
A prime $p$ is in class $1$ if the only prime divisors of $p+1$ are $2$ or $3$. In general, a prime $p$ is in class $r$ if every prime factor of $p+1$ is in some class $\leq r-1$, with equality for at least one prime factor.<br/><br/>Are there infinitely many primes in each class? If $p_r$ is the least prime in class $r$, then how does $p_r^{1/r}$ behave?
```

What is it?

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
For $n\geq 2k$ we define the deficiency of $\binom{n}{k}$ as follows. If $\binom{n}{k}$ is divisible by a prime $p\leq k$ then the deficiency is undefined. Otherwise, the deficiency is the number of $0\leq i<k$ such that $n-i$ is $k$-smooth, that is, divisible only by primes $\leq k$. <br/><br/>Are there infinitely many binomial coefficients with deficiency $1$? Are there only finitely many with deficiency $>1$?
```

### Problem 1100

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1100
- Status: open
- Prize: no

- OEIS: ["A325864", "possible"]
- Last edited: 19 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $1=d_1<\cdots<d_{\tau(n)}=n$ are the divisors of $n$, then let $\tau_\perp(n)$ count the number of $i$ for which $(d_i,d_{i+1})=1$.<br/><br/>Is it true that $\tau_\perp(n)/\omega(n)\to \infty$ for almost all $n$? Is it true that\[\tau_\perp(n)< \exp((\log n)^{o(1)})\]for all $n$?<br/><br/>Let\[g(k) = \max_{\omega(n)=k}\tau_\perp(n),\]where $\omega(n)$ counts the number of distinct prime divisors of $n$, and $n$ is restricted to squarefree integers. Determine the growth of $g(k)$.
```

### Problem 1101

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1101
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/1101.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 19 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
If $u=\{u_1<u_2<\cdots\}$ is a sequence of integers such that $(u_i,u_j)=1$ for all $i\neq j$ and $\sum \frac{1}{u_i}<\infty$ then let $\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}$ be the sequence of integers which are not divisible by any of the $u_i$. For any $x$ define $t_x$ by\[u_1\cdots u_{t_x}\leq x< u_1\cdots u_{t_x}u_{t_x+1}.\]We call such a sequence $u_i$ good if, for all $\epsilon>0$, if $x$ is sufficiently large then\[\max_{a_k<x} (a_{k+1}-a_k) < (1+\epsilon)t_x \prod_{i}\left(1-\frac{1}{u_i}\right)^{-1}.\]Is there a good sequence such that $u_n< n^{O(1)}$? Is there a good sequence such that $u_n\leq e^{o(n)}$?
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
We say that $A\subseteq \mathbb{N}$ has property $P$ if, for all $n\geq 1$, there are only finitely many $a\in A$ such that $n+a$ is squarefree.<br/><br/>We say that $A$ has property $Q$ if there are infinitely many $n$ such that $n+a$ is squarefree for all $a<n$. <br/><br/>How fast must sequences $A=\{a_1<a_2<\cdots\}$ with properties $P$ or $Q$ increase?
```

### Problem 1105

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1105
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/1105.lean
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 31 October 2025

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
The anti-Ramsey number $\mathrm{AR}(n,G)$ is the maximum possible number of colours in which the edges of $K_n$ can be coloured without creating a rainbow copy of $G$ (i.e. one in which all edges have different colours).<br/><br/>Let $C_k$ be the cycle on $k$ vertices. Is it true that\[\mathrm{AR}(n,C_k)=\left(\frac{k-2}{2}+\frac{1}{k-1}\right)n+O(1)?\]Let $P_k$ be the path on $k$ vertices and $\ell=\lfloor\frac{k-1}{2}\rfloor$. If $n\geq k\geq 5$ then is $\mathrm{AR}(n,P_k)$ equal to\[\max\left(\binom{k-2}{2}+1, \binom{\ell-1}{2}+(\ell-1)(n-\ell+1)+\epsilon\right)\]where $\epsilon=1$ if $k$ is odd and $\epsilon=2$ otherwise?
```

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text

### Problem 1165

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1165
- Status: solved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 27 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Given a random walk $s_0,s_1,\ldots$ in $\mathbb{Z}^2$, starting at the origin, let $f_n(x)$ count the number of $0\leq k\leq n$ such that $s_k=x$.<br/><br/>Let\[F(n)=\{ x: f_n(x) = \max_y f_n(y)\}\]be the set of 'favourite values'. Find\[\mathbb{P}(\lvert F(n)\rvert=r\textrm{ infinitely often})\]for $r\geq 3$.
```

### Problem 1166

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1166
- Status: proved
- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures
- OEIS: ["possible"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Given a random walk $s_0,s_1,\ldots$ in $\mathbb{Z}^2$, starting at the origin, let $f_k(x)$ count the number of $0\leq l\leq k$ such that $s_l=x$.<br/><br/>Let\[F(k)=\{ x: f_k(x) = \max_y f_k(y)\}\]be the set of 'favourite values'. Is it true that\[\left\lvert \bigcup_{k\leq n}F(k)\right\rvert \leq (\log n)^{O(1)}\]almost surely, for all but finitely many $n$?
```

### Problem 1167

- Source: https://www.erdosproblems.com/1167
- Status: open
- Prize: no
- Formalized: 1
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures/blob/main/FormalConjectures/ErdosProblems/1167.lean
- OEIS: ["N/A"]
- Last edited: 23 January 2026

#### Statement (verbatim)

```text
Let $r\geq 2$ be finite and $\lambda$ be an infinite cardinal. Let $\kappa_\alpha$ be cardinals for all $\alpha<\gamma$. <br/><br/>Is it true that\[2^\lambda \to (\kappa_\alpha+1)_{\alpha<\gamma}^{r+1}\]implies\[\lambda \to (\kappa_\alpha)_{\alpha<\gamma}^{r}?\]Here $+$ means cardinal addition, so that $\kappa_\alpha+1=\kappa_\alpha$ if $\kappa_\alpha$ is infinite.<br/>
```

- Prize: no
- Lean: https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures

```text
